\Lbrack\partial^{\alpha},{{\bf P}{\mathbf{M}}}\Rbrack
and
\Lbrack\partial^{\alpha},\mathcal{L}{\mathbf{M}}\Rbrack
. By the definition of the operator
{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}
, we can conclude that there exists a polynomial
\mathcal{U}(\cdot)
with
\mathcal{U}(0)=0
such that

\displaystyle\|\Lbrack\partial^{\alpha},{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}\Rbrack f\|_{\bf D}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha|-1,\infty}})\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}f\|^{2}_{H^{|\alpha|-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha|-1,\infty}})\|f\|^{2}_{H^{|\alpha|-1}}.

Furthermore, by using (
2.1
) and Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality with
\varepsilon>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\Lbrack\partial^{\alpha},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}\Rbrack({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f,\partial^{\alpha}f\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha|-1,\infty}})\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f\|_{H^{|\alpha|-1}_{\bf D}}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\bf D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha|-1,\infty}})\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f\|_{H^{|\alpha|-1}_{\bf D}}\big(\|\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f\|_{\bf D}+\|\partial^{\alpha}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}f\|_{\bf D}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\|\partial^{\alpha}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha|-1,\infty}})\Big(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})f\|_{H^{|\alpha|-1}_{\bf D}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f\|^{2}_{H^{|\alpha|}}\Big).

Combining the aforementioned estimates yields the expression for (
2.11
).
∎

2.1.2.
The nonlinear collision operators

Recalling that the nonlinear collision operators
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}
are defined as

\displaystyle\Gamma(f,g)=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{C}(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}f,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}g)

and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f,g)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f,\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}g)

respectively, we now focus on the trilinear estimates of these operators. The first result is concerned with estimates of
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}
without velocity weight.

Lemma 2.4
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\big|\big(\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma(f,g),\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}+\tilde{\alpha}=\alpha}\Big(\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{\bf D}+\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{\bf D}

(2.13)

and

\displaystyle\big|\big(\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f,g),\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}+\tilde{\alpha}=\alpha}\Big(\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{\bf D}+\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{\bf D}

(2.14)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}+\tilde{\alpha}+\overline{\alpha}=\alpha}{\bf 1}_{|\overline{\alpha}|\geq 1}\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\overline{\alpha}|-1,\infty}})\Big(\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{\bf D}+\big|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}g\big|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{\bf D}.

Here and in the sequel,
{\bf 1}_{\Omega}
is the usual indicator function of the set
\Omega
.

Proof.

Using
[
27
, Lemma 3, p. 406]
with
\theta=0
and
\beta=0
gives us (
2.13
). Since (
2.4
) is a special case of (
2.16
) below, we’ll omit the details for brevity. This concludes the proof of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

The following lemma is dedicated to trilinear estimates involving the nonlinear operators
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}
, incorporating velocity weights and velocity derivatives.

Lemma 2.5
.

(i)

For any
\ell\geq|\alpha|
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\big|\big(\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma(f,g),w^{2}_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\big|

(2.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha}\Big(\big|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g|_{\bf D}+\big|\langle v\rangle^{\ell-|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h|_{\bf D}.

(ii)

Let
q\in[0,1)
and
\ell\geq 0
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\big|\big(\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f,g),{\langle v\rangle}^{2(\ell-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\big)\big|

(2.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}=\alpha\atop{\beta^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime\prime}\leq\beta}}\Big(\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime}|)}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime\prime}|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}g\big|_{\bf D}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime}|)}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\tilde{\beta}|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\tilde{\beta}}g\big|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\big|_{\bf D}

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime\prime}|\leq|\alpha|-m-1\atop{\beta^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime\prime}\leq\beta}}{\bf 1}_{m\geq 0}\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{m,\infty}})\Big(\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime}|)}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}f\big|_{L^{2}}\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime\prime}|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}g\big|_{\bf D}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime}|)}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}}f\big|_{\bf D}\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta^{\prime\prime}|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime\prime}}g\big|_{L^{2}}\Big)\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\big|_{\bf D}.

Proof.

(
2.15
) directly follows from
[
69
, Lemma 10, p. 327]
.
We will only prove (
2.16
) here. To do this, we first denote
W={\langle v\rangle}^{(\ell-|\beta|)}\mathbf{M}^{-q/2}
for simplicity, and then write

\displaystyle\big|\big(\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f,g),W^{2}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\big)\big|=\sum C_{\alpha}^{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}}C_{\beta}^{\beta^{\prime}}G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}},

where
G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{6}G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{i}
with

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{1}=-\left(W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}f\right\}\right)\partial_{j}\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g,\partial_{i}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{2}=\left(W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\partial_{j}f\right\}\right)\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g,\partial_{i}\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{3}=-\left(W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{v_{i}-u_{i}}{2RT}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}f\right\}\right)\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{j}g,\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{4}=\left(W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{v_{i}-u_{i}}{2RT}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\partial_{j}f\right\}\right)\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g,\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{5}=-\left(\partial_{i}W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}f\right\}\right)\partial_{j}\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g,\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right),

\displaystyle G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{6}=\left(\partial_{i}W^{2}\left(\phi^{ij}\ast\partial_{\beta^{\prime}}\left\{\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\partial_{j}f\right\}\right)\partial_{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}g,\partial^{\alpha}_{\beta}h\right).

If
\alpha^{\prime\prime}=0
, since it follows from (
1.26
) that

\rho\sim 1,\ u\sim 0,\ T\sim T_{c},

then performing the same calculation as in the proof of
[
69
, Lemma 10, p. 327]
, one can see that all of
G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{i}

(1\leq i\leq 6)
can be controlled by the first part on the R.H.S. of (
2.16
).

If
|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|>0
, using (
1.13
) again, we obtain

\left|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|\leq C\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|,\infty}})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{3}{8}}

and

\left|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}\left\{\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{v_{i}-u_{i}}{2RT}\right\}\right|\leq C\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|,\infty}})\mathbf{M}^{\frac{3}{8}}.

From the above estimates and by repeating the similar calculations as those used to derive
[
69
, Lemma 10, p. 327]
, one can see that all of
G_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime},\beta^{\prime}}^{i}

(1\leq i\leq 6)
can be bounded by the second part of the right-hand side of (
2.16
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
2.5
.
∎

2.1.3.
The difference operator
\mathcal{L}_{d}

The subsequent lemma focuses on estimates associated with the difference operator
\mathcal{L}_{d}.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
\rho(t,x),u(t,x)
, and
T(t,x)
satisfy (
1.13
), and assume that (
1.26
) is valid. Define the weight function
w_{i}
as in (
1.24
). Then the following estimates hold:

i)

For any multi-index
\alpha
and the corresponding weight
w_{|\alpha|}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h],w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\big\|wh\big\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{\bf D}}^{2}.

(2.17)

ii)

If we set
\sqrt{\mathbf{M}}f=\sqrt{\mu}h
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{d}h,\partial^{\alpha}h\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\Big(\big\|({\bf I}-{\bf P})h\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{\bf D}}^{2}+\big\|f\|_{H^{|\alpha|}_{\bf D}}^{2}\Big).

(2.18)

iii)

There exists a positive constant
\delta
such that for
\alpha
and the corresponding weight
w_{|\alpha|}
:

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}[h],w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\geq

\displaystyle\delta\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{\bf D}^{2}-C\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha}|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(2.19)

Proof.

By (
1.22
), we can rewrite it as

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{d}f=\Gamma\left(\frac{\mathbf{M}-\mu}{\sqrt{\mu}},f\right)+\Gamma\left(f,\frac{\mathbf{M}-\mu}{\sqrt{\mu}}\right).

(2.20)

From (
1.26
), we have

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}\mathbf{M}|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\mu^{5/6}

(2.21)

for any multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|>0.
Moreover, using (
1.13
) and the mean value theorem, we get

\displaystyle|\mathbf{M}-\mu|=\left|[\nabla_{[\rho,u,T]}\mathbf{M}](\bar{\rho},\bar{u},\bar{T})\cdot(\rho-1,u,T-T_{c})\right|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\mu^{5/6},

(2.22)

where
\bar{\rho}\in(\min{\rho,1},\max{\rho,1})
,
\bar{u}_{j}\in(\min{u_{j},0},\max{0,u_{j}})
with
1\leq j\leq 3
, and
\bar{T}\in(T_{c},T).

Combining (
2.21
) and (
2.22
), we obtain

\Big|\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{M}-\mu)\Big]\Big|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(2.23)

With (
2.20
), (
2.23
), and (
2.15
) from Lemma
2.5
, we can derive (
2.17
).

Once (
2.17
) is established, we can derive (
2.18
) from

\displaystyle h=\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}f

(2.24)

and the fact that

\displaystyle\left\|\partial^{\alpha}{\bf P}\left[h^{\varepsilon}\right]\right\|^{2}_{\bf D}\lesssim\left\|\partial^{\alpha}{\bf P}\left[h^{\varepsilon}\right]\right\|^{2}\lesssim\left\|f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}_{H^{|\alpha|}}.

To complete the proof of (
2.19
), we use
[
69
, Lemma 9, p. 323]
with
q=1/(8\ln(\mathrm{e}+t))
to obtain

\displaystyle\big(\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}h,w_{|\alpha|}^{2}\partial^{\alpha}h\big)\geq

\displaystyle\delta\big|w_{|\alpha|}\partial^{\alpha}h\big|_{\bf D}^{2}-C(\eta)\big|\partial^{\alpha}h\big|^{2}_{L^{2}(B_{C(\eta)})}.

This, combined with (
2.24
), yields (
2.19
). The proof of Lemma
2.6
is now complete.
∎

2.2.
Estimates of
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}

In this subsection, our goal is to derive estimates for the remainders
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon}
, and
B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
, which satisfy the equations (
1.16
) and (
1.17
) with the initial data specified in (
•
‣
2
).

The global existence of (
1.16
), (
1.17
), and (
1.2
) with initial condition given by

\displaystyle\left[f^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v),E_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x),B_{R}^{\varepsilon}(0,x),h^{\varepsilon}(0,x,v)\right]=\left[f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v),E^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x),B^{\varepsilon}_{R,0}(x),h^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x,v)\right]

(2.25)

can be constructed through the local-in-time existence, the
a priori
energy estimate and the continuation argument.
Here, we focus on proving the a priori energy estimate (
1.34
) under the
a priori
assumption:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\kappa=\frac{1}{3}.

(2.26)

Before deriving the estimates for the remainders
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon}
, and
B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
, we first establish crucial estimates in the following lemmas. The first lemma deals with the velocity growth terms involving the electromagnetic field without velocity derivatives. Our strategy involves partitioning
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
into
\varepsilon
-dependent low-velocity and high-velocity regions. Subsequently, we estimate each region separately, utilizing the dissipation norms
\varepsilon^{-1}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}^{2}(i\leq 2)
and exploiting the relationship between
f^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Assume that
f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
and
h^{\varepsilon}
is the smooth solution to the Cauchy problem (
1.16
),(
1.17
), (
1.2
) and (
2.25
) for the VML system (
1.1
) and satisfies (
2.26
), then it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t),

(2.27)

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t),

(2.28)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t),

(2.29)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla^{i}_{x}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],2\pi T\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t),

(2.30)

for
i=1,2
, and
0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
with
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
, where

{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t):=\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}.

Proof.

First of all, we will focus on proving (
2.27
), while noting that (
2.28
), (
2.29
), and (
2.30
) can be established in a similar manner. We begin by considering the following:
From (
1.13
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big),\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\mathcal{U}(\|\nabla_{x}[\rho,u,T]\|_{W^{j,\infty}})\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|

(2.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\epsilon_{1}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|.

In order to eliminate the velocity growth in the right hand side of (
2.2
), we divide the domain of the
v-
integration
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
into
\varepsilon-
dependent low-velocity and high-velocity regions. This separation allows us to bound the microscopic and macroscopic components of the low-velocity part by
\varepsilon^{-1}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}^{2}
and
\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}
respectively. Simultaneously, the high velocity part can be controlled by
\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}
, facilitated by the choice of
T_{c}
in the global Maxwellian
\mu
.
More specifically, by (
1.26
), one has for suitably small
\epsilon_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\rho}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{T}{T_{c}}\Big)^{3/2}\exp\left(-\frac{(T-T_{c})|v|^{2}}{4RTT_{c}}+\frac{v\cdot u}{2RT}+\frac{|u|^{2}}{4RT}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{(T-T_{c})|v|^{2}}{8C_{0}RTT_{c}}\right)\lesssim C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{16C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}}\right),

which further implies that

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big)\right|\lesssim C_{\epsilon_{1}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}\right|\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{16C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}}\right).

(2.32)

Thus we get from (
2.32
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\leq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}{\langle v\rangle}^{3+4(i-j)}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\geq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}{\langle v\rangle}^{3+4(i-j)}\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}^{3/2+2(i-j)}\nabla_{x}^{j}{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{\bf D}^{2}

(2.33)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{i}C_{\epsilon_{1}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\langle v\rangle}^{4(i+1-j)}\geq\varepsilon^{-(i+1-j)}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}}\right)|h^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,dvdx

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\sum_{j=0}^{i}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{(i+1-j)}}\left\|\nabla_{x}^{j}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}\lesssim\,{\bf Z}_{1,i}(t).

∎

The next lemma is to control the velocity derivative terms associated with the Lorentz force. This aspect leads to more intricacies, particularly since our energy functional
\mathcal{E}(t)
lacks velocity derivative term. In addition to the techniques employed in Lemma
2.7
, our approach is to control
\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}
by using the dissipation norm
|\cdot|_{\bf D}
derived from the linearized Landau collision operator, which includes first-order velocity derivative terms.

Lemma 2.8
.

Under the assumptions in Lemma
2.7
, for
i=0,1,2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}{\bf Z}_{2,i}(t),

(2.34)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}{\bf Z}_{2,i}(t),

(2.35)

and for
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big[\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}+{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}{\bf Z}_{2,i}(t)\Big],

(2.36)

where

{\bf Z}_{2,i}(t):={\bf Z}_{1,i}(t)+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\Big(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\Big)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}.

Proof.

We only prove (
2.35
) for brevity, as analogous methods can be employed to derive (
2.34
) and (
2.8
).

Note that by the
a priori
assumption (
2.26
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(2.37)

Applying (
2.26
) and Sobolev’s inequalities, one has

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big(\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}\Big).

Then it suffices to control
|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}|^{2}
with
i=0,1,2.

To see this, as in (
2.32
), one has

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{8C_{0}RTT_{c}}\right)\left[{\langle v\rangle}^{2(i-j)+1}|h^{\varepsilon}|+{\langle v\rangle}^{2(i-j)}|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}|v|^{2}}{12C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}}\right)\big[|h^{\varepsilon}|+|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|\big].

Subsequently,
\left\|{\langle v\rangle}\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\right\|^{2}
can be further bounded by using similar arguments to those in (
2.2
). The difference lies in the inclusion of additional
\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}
norms to control terms
\sum_{j=0}^{i}|\nabla_{v}\nabla_{x}^{j}h^{\varepsilon}|
for
i=1,2
. This leads to the desired estimate (
2.35
).

∎

With the above estimates, we are now prepared to derive the following energy estimates for the remainders
[f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]
.

Proposition 2.1
.

Under the assumptions in Lemma
2.7
, it holds that for
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{i}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{i}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\delta\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i-1}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\big]

\displaystyle\times\Big(\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\left\|[E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]\right\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\left[\varepsilon^{2k+1+i}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+i}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}^{i}f^{\varepsilon}\|\right].

Proof.

Our proof is divided into three steps.

Step 1. Basic energy estimate of the remainders.
In this step, we derive the
L^{2}
estimates on
[f^{\varepsilon},E_{R}^{\varepsilon},B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]
. Taking the
L^{2}
inner product of (
1.16
) with
4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}
, we use (
2.8
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{1}{2}\big|\big\langle\partial_{t}Tf^{\varepsilon},4\pi Rf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\Big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot u\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},4\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})\big],4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot(v-u)f^{\varepsilon},2\pi f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

Here we have used the following identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}(t)\|^{2}\right)=4\pi\Big\langle v\cdot E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}},f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle

from (
1.17
).

Now, let’s estimate the terms on the right-hand side (R.H.S.) of (
2.39
) individually.

By (
1.13
), we can observe that the first two terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.39
) are bounded by

\displaystyle C(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\epsilon_{1}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big).

Considering Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.8
, we can control the 3rd, 5th, 7th-9th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.39
) as follows:

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big]\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+{\bf Z}_{1,0}(t)\Big).

For the 5th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.39
), we use (
2.4
), Sobolev’s inequality, and (
2.26
) to obtain

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}

(2.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}\lesssim\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

For the 6th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.39
), we can use (
2.4
) and (
4.1
) to derive the following:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},f^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})\big],4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}(1+t)^{n}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big(\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}+\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}\Big)\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon(1+t)^{2}\|{{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}}}[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2},

where we also used the fact that
0\leq t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
with
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
.

For the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.39
), we can apply Cauchy’s inequality, (
4.1
), and (
4.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RTf^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k+1\\
2\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{2(i+j-k-1)}\|F_{i}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\|F_{j}\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j\geq 2k\\
1\leq i,j\leq 2k-1\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{i+j-k}\Big(\|E_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|B_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)\big\|(1+v)\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{v}F_{j}\big\|\|f^{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Substituting the above estimates into (
2.39
) leads to:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\Big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\Big]\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)

(2.42)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Step 2. Estimates on the first order derivative of the remainders.

In this step, we proceed with the estimation of
\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}
,
\nabla_{x}E^{\varepsilon}
, and
\nabla_{x}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}
. To do this, applying
\partial^{\alpha}
(
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
) to (
1.16
) gives

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\partial^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}+\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\frac{\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)}{RT}\cdot\big(v-u\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]-\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]+\frac{\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}f^{\varepsilon}\Big[\partial_{t}+\hat{p}\cdot\nabla_{x}-\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}\Big]\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]+\varepsilon^{k-1}\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}[\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},f^{\varepsilon})+\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})\big]+\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]

(2.43)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{\big(v-u\big)}{2RT}f^{\varepsilon}\Big]+\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}_{0}.

Furthermore, by taking the inner product of
4\pi RT\partial^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}
and (
2.2
) with
|\alpha|=1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|[\nabla_{x}E_{R}^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle

(2.44)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\big|\big\langle\partial_{t}T\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon},2\pi R\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\sum_{|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=1}\Big|\Big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times\partial^{\alpha}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}\Big[\frac{v-u}{T}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{|\alpha|=1}\big|\big\langle v\big(\nabla_{x}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\left[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\right],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})\big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

For the 2nd term on the L.H.S. of (
2.44
), applying (
2.11
) and (
1.13
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\delta}{4\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}-\frac{C\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{C\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\big(\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big).

Now, let’s estimate the terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) separately.

Referring again to (
1.13
), the upper bound of the 1st to 3rd terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) is

C\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big).

Lemmas
2.7
and
2.8
show that the 4th-6th, 9th-12th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) are dominated by

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big]\Big[\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+{\bf Z}_{2,1}(t)\Big].

By applying (
2.4
), (
2.37
), and Sobolev’s inequalities, we can bound the 7th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) by

\displaystyle C\Big[\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big)\Big].

Similarly, by virtue of (
4.1
), the upper bound of the 8th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) is

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+C\Big[\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big].

For the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
), we can further use (
4.2
) to have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RT\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Finally, by plugging the above estimates into (
2.44
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big[\varepsilon\Big(

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)\Big]+\delta\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.45)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\Big((1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\Big)\Big[\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}\Big)\Big]+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+2}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+1}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Step 3. Estimates on the second order derivative of the remainders.

In this step, we proceed to derive the estimate of
\|[\nabla^{2}_{x}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}^{2}E_{R}^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}^{2}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}]\|
. For results in this direction, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big[\varepsilon^{2}

\displaystyle\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)\Big]+\delta\varepsilon\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}\Big((1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\Big)\Big[\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{1}}\exp\left(-\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{8C_{0}RT^{2}_{c}\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\Big(\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\Big)\Big]+\epsilon_{1}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+\varepsilon^{2k+3}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+2}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|.

To prove (
2.46
), we first take the inner product of
4\pi RT\partial^{\alpha}f^{\varepsilon}
and (
2.2
) for
|\alpha|=2
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(\|\sqrt{4\pi RT}\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|\nabla_{x}^{2}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\;\big|\big\langle\partial_{t}T\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon},2\pi R\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\sum_{|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=2}\Big|\Big\langle\partial^{\alpha}\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}\Big[\frac{v-u}{T}\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=2}\big|\big\langle v\partial^{\alpha}\big(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}f^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}^{2}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[f^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)\mathbf{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},f^{\varepsilon}),4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},f^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbf{M}}(f^{\varepsilon},\mathbf{M}^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})\big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

(2.47)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}f^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\frac{(v-u)}{2T}f^{\varepsilon}\Big],4\pi T\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

By using (
1.13
) and (
2.11
), the 2nd term on the L.H.S. of (
2.2
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{M}}[f^{\varepsilon}],4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\delta}{4\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}-\frac{C\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{C\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\Big(\varepsilon\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big).

It follows from (
1.13
) that the first three terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.2
) can be bounded by

C\Big[\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big]\Big[\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+{\bf Z}_{2,2}(t)\Big].
In view of Lemma
2.7
and Lemma
2.8
, the 4th-6th, 9th-12th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.2
) are no more than

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big]\Big[\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+{\bf Z}_{2,2}(t)\Big].

Similar to the corresponding terms in (
2.44
), the 7th and 8th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.44
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}+\frac{C\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}.

By using (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), we can further estimate the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.2
) as

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{Q}_{0},4\pi RT\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|\nabla_{x}({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{\mathbf{M}})[f^{\varepsilon}]\|_{\bf D}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|\nabla_{x}^{2}f^{\varepsilon}\|.

Substituting the aforementioned estimates into (
2.2
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{2}
gives us (
2.46
).

In conclusion, (
2.1
) follows from (
2.2
), (
2.45
), and (
2.46
).
∎

2.3.
Estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}

Before proceeding to derive the energy estimates for
h^{\varepsilon}
, let’s establish some lemmas for later use. The first lemma deals with the velocity growth terms involving the electric field. In contrast to Lemma
2.7
for the corresponding estimates of
f^{\varepsilon}
, we control these velocity growth terms using additional dissipation terms
Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}
. These terms arise due to the special weight function
\exp\left(\frac{\langle v\rangle^{2}}{8RT_{c}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}\right)
. Additionally, the estimates make use of the terms
\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}
for
i=0,1,2
and rely on the favorable time-decay characteristics described in (
1.13
) for
[\rho,u,T]
as well as the smallness of
\varepsilon
.

Lemma 2.9
.

Under the assumptions in Lemma
2.7
, for
i=0,1,2
, the following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\epsilon_{1}(1+t)^{-p_{0}}{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}\varepsilon\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}}.

For
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}{\bf 1}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}.

(2.48)

Proof.

To verify (
2.48
), we will focus on this particular inequality, as the other two can be handled in a similar manner.

Using (
1.13
), for
t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}(1+t)^{n}\Big(\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon(1+t)\Big(\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}}.

∎

The following lemma is dedicated to controlling the velocity derivative terms associated with the Lorentz force. Since the energy functional
\mathcal{E}(t)
defined in (
1.27
) does not include any terms involving velocity derivatives, we need to bound the velocity derivative terms using the dissipation norm
\|\cdot\|^{2}_{\bf D}
. However, this norm becomes degenerate for large velocities, and we must combine it with additional dissipative terms induced by our special weight functions to compensate for the velocity degeneracy.

Additionally, we need to deal with terms like
\varepsilon^{k}\big(v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}
, where both the velocity
v
and a velocity derivative are involved. Fortunately, as demonstrated in Lemma
2.2
, the velocity degeneracy for
({\bf I}-{\bf P}v)[\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}]
is of order
1
in
|\cdot|^{2}_{\bf D}
, in contrast to the order
3
degeneracy of
{\bf P}_{v}[\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}]
. Although the
|v|^{2}
growth in the dissipation norms
Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}(i=0,1,2)
may not fully compensate for the velocity degeneracy due to the possible order
3
degeneracy of
{\bf P}_{v}[\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}]
, we overcome this difficulty by specifically designing the polynomial velocity weight part
\langle v\rangle^{\ell-i}
(where
i
is the order of
x
-derivatives) to compensate for the weak dissipation of the linearized Landau operator.

However, this polynomial velocity weight also leads to velocity growth when estimating the nonlinear collision operator
\varepsilon^{k-1}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})
. Furthermore, these growths can be absorbed by the exponential weight, as indicated in (
2.49
), where we ensure that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big[{\langle v\rangle}^{i}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle^{2}}{8C_{0}RT_{c}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}\right)\Big]\lesssim\Big(\frac{\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}{\epsilon_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{i}{2}}.

(2.49)

Further details on this can be found in the proof of Proposition
2.2
, particularly in inequalities like (
2.57
) and (
2.58
).

Lemma 2.10
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
2.7
, for
i=0,1,2
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E+v\times B\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}\Big(Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}_{\bf D}}\Big),

(2.50)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\varepsilon\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}.

(2.51)

For
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\big[\big(E_{n}

\displaystyle+v\times B_{n}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\big],w_{i}^{2}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

(2.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i-1}_{\bf D}}\Big).

Proof.

We will prove (
2.51
) and omit the proof of (
2.50
) and (
2.52
) for the sake of brevity. For (
2.51
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=i\\
|\alpha^{\prime}|<i\end{subarray}}{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\big|\big\langle\partial^{\alpha}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{v}\big(w^{2}_{i}\big)\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon},\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=i\\
|\alpha^{\prime}|<i\end{subarray}}{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\big|\big\langle\big(v\times\partial^{\alpha}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\big({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{v})[\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}],w^{2}_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=i\\
|\alpha^{\prime}|<i\end{subarray}}{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|\partial^{\alpha}E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}}\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{-1}w_{i}\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{3}_{x}}\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}|=i\\
|\alpha^{\prime}|<i\end{subarray}}{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|\partial^{\alpha}B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{6}}\Big\|\frac{|v|}{{\langle v\rangle}}w_{i}\big({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{v})[\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}]\Big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{3}_{x}}\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

Furthermore, by Lemma
2.2
, for
|\alpha^{\prime}|<i
with
1\leq i\leq 2
, we can get that

\displaystyle\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{-1}w_{i}\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{3}_{x}}^{2}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}{\langle v\rangle}^{-2}w_{i}^{2}|\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}|^{2}_{L^{3}_{x}}dv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\Big({\langle v\rangle}^{-\frac{3}{2}}w_{i}|\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{6}_{x}}\Big)\Big({\langle v\rangle}^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{i}|\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}_{x}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|w_{i-1}\nabla_{x}^{|\alpha^{\prime}|}h^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bf D}\left\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{|\alpha^{\prime}|+1}h^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bf D}.

And similarly,

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{|v|}{{\langle v\rangle}}w_{i}\big({\bf I}-{\bf P}_{v})[\nabla_{v}\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}h^{\varepsilon}]\Big\|_{L^{2}_{v}L^{3}_{x}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|w_{i-1}\nabla_{x}^{|\alpha^{\prime}|}h^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bf D}\left\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{|\alpha^{\prime}|+1}h^{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\bf D}.

The two aforementioned estimates are the very reason why we design our weight functions
w_{i}
to depend on the order
i
of spatial derivatives.

Noting that
(1+t)^{-\beta}\lesssim Y(t)
and
\varepsilon\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y(t)
for
t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
, we can further use (
2.37
) to get

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{i}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}\Big)\Big(\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+{\bf I}_{i\geq 1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i-1}_{\bf D}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\varepsilon\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}.

∎

We have now reached the stage of applying weighted energy estimates to the function
h^{\varepsilon}
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
2.7
, we can establish the following weighted energy estimate for
h^{\varepsilon}
with
\kappa=\frac{1}{3}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}

\displaystyle\big(\varepsilon^{i+1+\kappa}\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i+1+\kappa}Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\delta\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i+\kappa}\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}

(2.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i+\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{i+1+2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\sum\limits_{i=0}^{1}\varepsilon^{i+1+\kappa}\Big((1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}^{2}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}_{\bf D}}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{2k+i+2+\kappa}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{2}\varepsilon^{k+i+1+\kappa}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{i}\nabla_{x}^{i}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

Proof.

The proof is divided into three steps.

Step 1. Basic Weighted Energy Estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
.
In this step, we deduce the weighted
L^{2}
estimate of
h^{\varepsilon}
. For results in this direction, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}

\displaystyle\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}Y\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\delta\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}

(2.54)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\varepsilon^{1+2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big)+\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+2+\kappa}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+1+\kappa}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

To prove this, we first take the
L^{2}
inner product of
w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}
with (
1.2
) and use (
2.19
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}

\displaystyle+Y\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.55)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\Big|\Big\langle\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{RT}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\Big|\Big\langle\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},h^{\varepsilon})+\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})],w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

We now proceed to estimate the terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.55
) term by term.

From (
2.17
), the upper bound of the 2nd term on the R.H.S. of (
2.55
) is
C\epsilon_{1}\varepsilon^{-1}|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}]|^{2}_{\bf D}
. Noting that
{\langle v\rangle}w_{0}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}
is bounded, the 3rd term can be bounded by
\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+C\varepsilon\big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)
. Applying Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.10
, we can control the 4th, 5th, and 8th-11th terms by

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+C\epsilon_{1}Y\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\Big).

For the 6th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.55
),
we get from (
2.15
) and Sobolev’s inequality that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big(\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big|_{L^{2}}|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}|_{\bf D}+|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big|_{\bf D}\Big)|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}|_{\bf D}dx\,

(2.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\Big(\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}\Big).

Here, we’ve used the Sobolev’s inequality
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{2}_{x}}
to bound both
\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big|_{L^{2}}
and
\big|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big|_{\bf D}
, as either
|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}|_{L^{2}}
or
|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}|_{\bf D}
cannot accommodate more
x-
derivatives. Note that in norms
\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}
and
\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}
,
\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}
can only bear the polynomial weight
{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell-2}
and an additional polynomial weight
{\langle v\rangle}^{2}
arises. We replace
{\langle v\rangle}^{2}
with the exponential weight
\exp\left(\frac{\langle v\rangle^{2}}{8RT_{c}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}\right)
to obtain

\displaystyle\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big[{\langle v\rangle}^{2}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle^{2}}{8C_{0}RT_{c}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}\right)\Big]\big\|wh^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}.

(2.57)

Similarly, for the 7th term, we have

\displaystyle\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\Big[{\langle v\rangle}^{2}\exp\left(\frac{-\epsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle^{2}}{8C_{0}RT_{c}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}\right)\Big]\big\|wh^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}.

(2.58)

Moreover, for
k\geq 3
, it follows from the
a priori
assumption (
2.26
) that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{i}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{k-2-\frac{i}{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-\frac{i}{2}}.

(2.59)

Thus, by using
t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
and the assumption
\varepsilon\ll\epsilon_{1}
, we can plug (
2.57
) and (
2.58
) into (
2.3
) to get

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\ln^{2}(\mathrm{e}+t)\varepsilon^{3}\big\|wh^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}.

For the 7th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.55
), we use
(
4.1
) and Lemma
2.5
to bound it by

\displaystyle C\sum_{i=1}^{2k-1}[\varepsilon(1+t)]^{i-1}(1+t)\Big(\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|+\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}\Big)\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\kappa}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}.

Similarly, for the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.55
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

Putting these estimates into (
2.55
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}
yields (
2.54
).

Step 2. First order derivative estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}
with weight.

In this step, we continue deducing the estimate of
\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|
. We apply
\partial^{\alpha}
for
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
to (
1.2
), resulting in the following equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\partial^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\partial^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}+\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\frac{\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)}{RT}\cdot\big(v-u\big)\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}\Big]

\displaystyle+\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big]-\partial^{\alpha}\big[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\big]+\frac{\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}[h^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}]}{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{k-1}\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon})+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}[\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},h^{\varepsilon})+\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big]-\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big]

(2.60)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\partial^{\alpha}\Big[\Big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big]

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\partial^{\alpha}\Big(\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big)+\varepsilon^{k}\partial^{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}_{1}.

Next, we take the
L^{2}
inner product of
w^{2}_{1}\partial^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}
with (
2.3
), considering
|\alpha|=1
, and apply (
2.19
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{RT}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}\Big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\big[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},h^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big[\big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}\Big(\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big),w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

Now let’s estimate the R.H.S. of equation (
2.61
) individually.

The upper bound for the 2nd term on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
) is

C\epsilon_{1}\varepsilon^{-1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}.

The 3rd term on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
) can be bounded by

o(1)\varepsilon^{-1}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{\bf D}+C\varepsilon\big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big).

Using Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.10
, we can bound the 4th, 5th, and 8th to 11th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
) by

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\big]\big(\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)+\epsilon_{1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}\Big].

Regarding the 6th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
), one can use (
2.15
) and Sobolev’s inequalities to bound it by

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{k-1}\Big(\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell-1}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell-1}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}\Big).

On the other hand, similar to (
2.57
) and (
2.58
), one has

\displaystyle\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell-1}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}},\quad\big\|{\langle v\rangle}^{\ell-1}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\ln(\mathrm{e}+t)}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}.

Then we can further bound this term by

o(1)\varepsilon^{-1}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+C\varepsilon\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}
.

For the 7th term on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
), considering (
4.1
) and Lemma
2.5
, we can establish its upper bound as

o(1)\varepsilon^{-1}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{\kappa}\big(\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{\bf D}\big)

for
t\leq\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
.

Similarly, for the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.61
), it can be estimated as

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\varepsilon}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

Plugging these estimates into (
2.61
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}Y\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\delta\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}

(2.62)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{1+\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{2+2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\Big)+\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}\Big((1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\|w_{0}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{\bf D}\Big)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+3+\kappa}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+2+\kappa}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{1}\nabla_{x}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

Step 3. Second order derivative estimates of
h^{\varepsilon}
with weight.

In this final step, we proceed to derive the following estimate for
\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}

\displaystyle\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}Y\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\delta\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}

(2.63)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2+\kappa}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\varepsilon^{3+2\kappa}\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\epsilon_{1}\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}\Big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2k+4+\kappa}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k+3+\kappa}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

To demonstrate this, we first take the
L^{2}
inner product of
w^{2}_{2}\partial^{\alpha}h^{\varepsilon}
and (
2.3
) with
|\alpha|=2
and use (
2.19
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+Y(t)\big\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}

(2.64)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|f^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{d}[h^{\varepsilon}],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\frac{v-u}{RT}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{M}\Big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\frac{E\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\big[\big(E+v\times B\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k-1}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},h^{\varepsilon}),w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n-1}\big|\big\langle[\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n},h^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{x}^{2}\Gamma(h^{\varepsilon},\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}F_{n})],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{k}\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\Big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|+\varepsilon^{k}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\frac{E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big[\big(E_{n}+v\times B_{n}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varepsilon}+\big(E_{R}^{\varepsilon}+v\times B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\big)\cdot\nabla_{v}F_{n}\Big],w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{2k-1}\varepsilon^{n}\Big|\Big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\Big(\frac{E_{n}\cdot v}{2RT_{c}}h^{\varepsilon}\Big),w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\Big\rangle\Big|+\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|.

Now, let’s estimate the R.H.S. of (
2.64
) term by term.

For the 2nd and 3rd terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.64
), their upper bounds are

C\epsilon_{1}\varepsilon^{-1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}+C\varepsilon\Big(\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\Big).

For the 4th, 5th, 8th-11th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.64
), based on Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.10
, we can bound them as follows:

\displaystyle C\Big[\epsilon_{1}Y(t)\|{\langle v\rangle}w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}+\big[(1+t)^{-p_{0}}+\varepsilon^{2\kappa}\big]\big(\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|E_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|B_{R}^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}\big)+\epsilon_{1}\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}\Big].

Regarding the 6th and 7th terms on the R.H.S. of (
2.64
),
from (
2.15
), (
4.1
), (
2.59
), and Sobolev’s inequalities, we can control them by

\displaystyle C\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|_{H^{2}_{\bf D}}^{2}+\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|_{\bf D}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{\kappa}\Big(\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|wh^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\bf D}}\Big).

Similarly, for the last term on the R.H.S. of (
2.64
), we can express it as

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\nabla_{x}^{2}\mathcal{Q}_{1},w^{2}_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim\frac{o(1)}{\varepsilon}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}]\|^{2}_{\bf D}+\varepsilon^{2k+1}(1+t)^{4k+2}+\varepsilon^{k}(1+t)^{2k}\|w_{2}\nabla_{x}^{2}h^{\varepsilon}\|.

By substituting these estimates into (
2.64
) and multiplying the resulting inequality by
\varepsilon^{3+\kappa}
, we obtain (
2.63
).

Finally, (
2.53
) follows from (
2.54
), (
2.62
), and (
2.63
).
∎

2.4.
